Opening a client in Content Review reads the row again and refills the settings, leaving a field somebody has already changed as they typed it; opening a client record reads the row again and repaints where it has moved on; entering Content Review reloads its list. `tests/datefloor.js` drives both directions. |
| A sheet opens at its top, every time (`js/sheet.js`, 2026-09-26) | **The same sheet serves Add and Edit and is only hidden between uses, so its body kept the scroll it was left at**: a lead added after a record was edited opened part way down, on Source and Industry with the brand name out of sight, and Billing's Edit did the same (reported by the user). `ADspaceSheet.show()` puts the sheet and its `.sheet-body` back at the top and focuses the card with `preventScroll`, and a `MutationObserver` does the same for every `.sheet` any section unhides directly (the confirmation sheets, My Work's own sheets, the report sheets). `tests/datefloor.js` §3 fails without it. |
| Person in charge follows a stand-down and a rename (`js/team.js` `ownedBy` / `handOver`, 2026-09-26) | **`clients.owner` and `campaigns.owner` hold the person's name as text, so the Team page follows through.** Set inactive counts what the person is in charge of (their clients, their campaigns not yet Completed, matched on the trimmed name in any case) and the same sheet asks **Person in charge from now on**, with **Keep with {name}** first because a stand-down can be for a week, and only active colleagues offered. A move writes each table with `.select('id')` and names a refusal, and files `client.edited` / `campaign.edited` under the record it changed, so it reads on that record's own Activity. A completed campaign keeps who ran it. **A rename carries the new name onto every client and campaign** that named them, completed campaigns included, without asking, because it is the same person. A colleague is never deleted from the Team page (stood down only), so there is no delete path to follow. `tests/team.js` drives the count, the choice, the move, Keep, the completed campaign and the rename; `tests/stub2.js` now applies an update scoped by `.in()`, as PostgREST does. |
| Passkeys for the console (`js/passkey.js`, `admin/index.html`, 2026-09-26) | **Sign in with a passkey** (Touch ID, Face ID or the device password) sits under the email's Sign in as a tonal button at its width, drawn only where the browser has WebAuthn and the library carries the calls. **The console alone loads supabase-js 2.117.2**, the first line with passkeys; the client pages stay on 2.45.4 until they need something it adds. Cancel in the system prompt says nothing; passkeys switched off, an unknown passkey, a second one on the same device, the captcha and a rate limit are each named in our words; the captcha token rides the call as it rides the email. **Passkeys** in the account menu opens a sheet: the list (name, Added, Last used), **Add a passkey**, and Rename and Remove in each row's ⋯, Remove asking first and saying the email still signs in. **Once per browser**, on a device that can hold one and to somebody who has none, the console asks after an email sign-in (**Add passkey** / **Not now**, remembered in `localStorage` as `adspace-passkey-offer`). The passkeys are Supabase's; nothing is stored in the portal's tables. Two shared fixes went with it: **a question over a sheet sits above it** (`#askSheet` z-index 95; every sheet is moved to the end of the page on open, so a confirmation could open underneath the sheet it was about), and **a ⋯ inside a phone sheet opens under its button** (`ADspaceMenu.place` takes the card's offset off: the phone card is `will-change: transform`, which makes it the box a fixed menu is placed in, so every such menu opened as far below its button as the card is from the top of the screen). The Supabase settings (RP ID `digital.adspace.me`, origin `https://digital.adspace.me`) are in `docs/SIGN-IN-SECURITY.md` §1a. `tests/passkey.js` drives all of it against the stand-in and loads the **real** 2.117.2 in front of the page (installed with `npm i --prefix tests/sbjs @supabase/supabase-js@2.117.2`, SKIP printed where it is not) to prove it draws the sign-in card and carries every passkey call. |
| A deleted user group stays deleted (`supabase/schema.sql`, `js/team.js`, 2026-09-26) | **Reported by the user: Sales was deleted and kept coming back.** The schema seeded Admin, Marketing and Sales with `on conflict (slug) do nothing` on every run, and a row that is gone raises no conflict, so each re-run of `supabase/schema.sql` put a deleted group back (the rate card's lesson, missed here). **Marketing and Sales are seeded only into an empty table; Admin alone is ensured on every run**, because the console cannot be run without it and its guard refuses its deletion. **The Team page's group Delete asks for the row back** (`.select('slug')`): an empty answer is a refusal and reads `Not deleted. The database refused the request.`, never `Sales deleted.` over a group that stayed. `tests/levels.js` §12 runs the seed against real Postgres empty, after a deletion, twice, and without Admin; `tests/team.js` drives the refused and the allowed delete. **Nothing to run by hand**: delete Sales again on the Team page and it stays gone. |
| The status bar is the bar's colour (`css/portal.css` `--chrome-solid`, `admin/index.html`, 2026-09-26) | **Safari on an iPhone paints the status bar from the sticky bar at the top of the page, and passes over a translucent one**, so the console's head at 88% white fell through to the grey page and read as a grey band above a white bar (reported with a screenshot). Under a coarse pointer `.console-head` and `.topbar` take `--chrome-solid` (white; `#171717` in dark, which is what the translucent bar already showed over the page) with no backdrop filter, so nothing on the screen changes but the band. The `theme-color` meta says the same colour for the Safari versions and the installed app that read it, and **follows the console's own theme** rather than the device's: the head script and `wearTheme()` rewrite it (the dark value was `#1f1f1f`, the card's, not the bar's). The client pages carry a white `theme-color` of their own. Not measurable in the sandbox (no Safari), so it is confirmed on a phone. |
| State in the URL | `?s=clients|review|campaigns|links|services|team`, `client=`, `campaign=`, `tab=`, `set=`, `new=`. A refresh (or switching tabs) lands where the person was with what they typed. Kept as `?s=` after weighing `/admin/crm` style paths. True path routing (`/admin/clients/hkl-lim-team`) on GitHub Pages means the root `404.html` catching every unknown address and rewriting it in script; that file is the portal's from 2026-09-21, so the door is no longer shut by ownership — it is shut by the trade. Every genuine miss would then be served by a page that runs a script to decide whether it is a miss at all, and the cost of getting that wrong is a portal that answers a wrong address with a blank screen instead of a cover. The address bar is not worth that. `client=` carries the client's readable `slug` (set once from the name, never following a rename, a clash numbered), and `clientByKey()` resolves a slug or an older UUID by the key's shape. The slug is in the console only: it is an address, never access. |

### Non-negotiable validation before "done"

**The behavioural suites are in `adspaceplt/digital-form-tests`, a private
repository.** This one is public, and the harness, the database schema and
these working notes were all reachable at digital.adspace.me and readable on
github.com; the suites were taken out on the user's instruction on 2026-09-20.
`_config.yml` stops Pages publishing the rest.

**They are cloned beside the portal and linked in**, which is the first thing
to do in a new session, before changing behaviour:

```
git clone https://github.com/adspaceplt/digital-form-tests /home/user/digital-form-tests
ln -sfn /home/user/digital-form-tests /home/user/digital-form/tests
```

The symlink is what lets every suite go on taking `tests` as its argument and
reading `tests/stub2.js` beside itself; `.gitignore` holds both `tests` and
`tests/`, because a symlink is not a directory and the trailing slash alone
does not match it. **A repair to a suite is committed and pushed to that
repository in the same batch as the change it covers**, or it is lost with the
container.

**Every suite the sweep below names now exists** (2026-09-21). `docs.js`,
`cmdbar.js`, `six.js` and `sch.js` were the last four that had been written
after `02de7c9` and never reached a commit anywhere; they are written again
and pass. `docs.js` needed the **Documents Register half of `tests/stub2.js`**,
which did not exist at all — `documents`, a seeded `doc_types`, the seven
functions, and the read policy in `builder()` so an HR row never arrives where
`register.hr` is not held. It also needs `npm i --prefix tests/pdfx`, and
prints `docs: SKIP` loudly where that has not been run.

**It was lost with the container, for three days, and that is why this section
reads the way it does.** Between 2026-09-20 and the move, the instruction here
was `git checkout 02de7c9 -- tests`, and every session dutifully restored,
repaired, ran and then deleted the folder — so the same repair was paid for
again each session and never survived one. By the time it was measured, that
restore point was eight pull requests behind the product: **eight suites
passed, thirteen crashed, and twelve had been written after `02de7c9` and
never reached a commit anywhere**, `ops.js` and `work.js` among them. Those
twelve are gone and are being written again; `STATUS.md` in that repository is
the live register of which suite is in which state, and is what to read before
claiming the gate ran.

Everything below still applies. What changed is where the suites live, not
whether they are run: a portal this size cannot be checked by reading it.

1. `node --check` on every changed `js/*.js`.
2. Local server: `setsid nohup npx --yes http-server -p 8899 -s . >/dev/null 2>&1 &` from the repo root (it dies between turns; restart on `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`).
3. The full behavioural sweep, every suite green. **Read the exit code, never a count of `FAIL` lines**: a suite that throws — a locator that times out because the control it names has been removed, a native time field filled with `2pm` — prints no `FAIL` line at all, so `grep -c '^FAIL'` reports zero and the sweep reads a crash as a pass. That is how `tests/crm.js` sat broken on `main`, and how `tests/prod.js` sat dead from its bulk-logistics section onward.
   `for s in run camp client cprod bar newbadge prod qr regress backup keyin state race chrome crm sgd team portal creator canvas register geom letter links sets setdel six sch undo levels trail docs work cmdbar qc perfui keys slide perms cprofile perfguard pwa reports adsreport signin phone camptime datefloor passkey; do out=$(node tests/$s.js tests 2>&1); rc=$?; if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then echo "$s: CRASHED rc=$rc"; else echo "$s: $(echo "$out" | tail -1)"; fi; done`
3a. **The wider matrix and the keyboard: `node tests/matrix.js tests` → `matrix: ok`.** A representative screen from every route at **320, 375, 390, 768, 1024, 1280 and 1440**, and again at 1280 and 1440 under **200% browser zoom** — the CSS viewport halved, not the `zoom` property, because Ctrl + is what a reader presses and it fires the media queries. It runs `uxaudit`'s own `inPage()` checks, read out of that file rather than copied, so a rule added there is a rule the matrix enforces, and drives the keyboard through every control that opens a dialog.
3b. **For any change to the operations system: `node tests/ops.js tests` → `ops: ok`.** It runs the dated migration against a throwaway Postgres 16, twice, with the real `allowed()` and `level_rank()` cut out of `supabase/schema.sql` rather than restated, and drives the permission gates, the stage machine, the commitment history, assignment, work sessions, revisions, the client's decision, completion, recurring generation and the append-only audit trail. It also compares each canonical schema section with its own migration byte for byte, cutting phase 1 at phase 2's banner: a cut with no end marker takes everything appended to the file afterwards. **The page is `node tests/work.js tests` → `work: ok`**, which drives the queue, the workspace, the stage machine and its refusals, the timer and the permission classes against `tests/stub2.js`.
3b2. **For any change to performance reviews: `node tests/perf.js tests` → `perf: ok`** (real Postgres, the migration twice, the mirror compared) **and `node tests/perfui.js tests` → `perfui: ok`** (both pages against `tests/stub2.js`, whose `perfRpc` mirrors every function and refusal, with a fixture code of its own that is never the live one).
3c. **For any change to `supabase/schema.sql`, including a seed or a function: `node tests/sql.js tests` → `sql: ok`.** It starts a throwaway Postgres 16 cluster and runs the real thing: the stage clock, the team sweep and repair, the overlap guard, the rate card seed, the whole creator delivery path, the client's recorded decision, the creator rating and the handle reader. Every other suite drives a browser against `stub2.js`, so PL/pgSQL is the one part of the portal nothing else checks. It builds its own cut-down tables, so a column added to the schema has to be added to that fixture too. It prints `SKIP` loudly where Postgres is missing rather than passing quietly.
4. `node tests/uxaudit.js tests` → must print `uxaudit: ok`. **Read that line, never the exit code**: the walk exits 0 on `uxaudit: PROBLEM (18 fail)` as well, and on 2026-09-22 a run with eighteen failures was read as green from its exit code and a PR opened on it. It walks every page and state at 1280 and at 390 with a coarse pointer, and the console again in dark at both widths, and fails on: sideways overflow, a cell alone on its row, uneven card padding, a header cell off its column (`cols`), the same cell starting at different x on different rows (`column`), a last column that ends short of the row's right edge on a phone (`edge`), uneven gaps between the blocks of a section (`stack`), text under 11px (`type`), a value that wraps or clips, buttons in one row at different heights (or widths on a phone), a control under 38px (44px touch; `.btn-sm`, `.input-sm`, `.select-sm` may be 32px on desktop), a field or icon-only button without a name, more than two filled actions in a view, text under 4.5:1, a control border under 3:1, a control that takes focus without a ring, and a row whose way out is drawn before its action (`order`). The review page's post mockups (`.card-stage`) are outside the audit. **A new route or pane is added to the walk in the same push that builds it**, and to `tests/matrix.js`'s route list: a screen nobody walks is a screen whose alignment, contrast and touch targets nobody measured, and the walk is the only thing that compares two pages against each other.
5. Screenshots at 1280 and 390 of every screen the change touches, in both themes when the change is visual (`SHOTS=1 node tests/uxaudit.js tests` for the whole walk into `tests/walk/`). Each one is opened and read against the phone checklist in `DESIGN.md`, not just taken.
6. For any PDF change: `node tests/pdfreal.js tests` and `node tests/pdfcases.js tests` → `pdfcases: ok`, which draws the letter against the extremes one fixture never reaches and asserts what only geometry can say — the four AcroForm fields exist as widgets with valid rectangles, the execution block is all on one page, and nothing drawn crosses the margins, read back from glyph positions with pdf.js because a clipped string decodes perfectly as text. `node tests/pdfshot.js tests <file.pdf>` rasterises a letter. Both need `npm i pdfjs-dist@3.11.174 --prefix tests/pdfx` and the local server.
7. Version stamp: every stylesheet and script tag carries `?v=YYYYMMDD` (`20260920a`, `b`… for further pushes the same day). Bump on every push that changes CSS or JS with one `sed` over every HTML file that carries one.
8. The deploy is confirmed from the GitHub Pages workflow run for the merge commit (`pages build and deployment`, conclusion success). `curl` to digital.adspace.me returns 403 from the sandbox and proves nothing.


## 4. Workflow and constraints

### Git and delivery
- Branch `cl/exciting-mayer-fvg0dc`; one PR per batch, squash-merged to `main` by Claude; after a merge the branch is reset onto `origin/main` (`git checkout -B … origin/main`, force-with-lease push). Never stack on merged history.
- Commit message: a one-line title in plain English about what the person gains, a body in the same register, then the two trailers from the session reminder. PR body: What changed, Test plan (ticked), the two footer lines. No model identifiers in any repo artifact.
- The user is in "proceed and merge" mode: build, test, push, open the PR, merge, confirm the Pages build, then report. Ask only when readings differ materially; never re-explain settled decisions.
- When a schema change ships, the report says "re-run `supabase/schema.sql`" in the user-side list. Edge functions (`sign-upload`, `invite-member`, Verify JWT off) are deployed by the user with the Supabase CLI.
- Never ask for a URL, key or asset the repo or config already holds (`brandLogo`, favicon, fonts in `css/`). Check `js/config.js` and `css/` first.

### Folder and file rules
- **`index.html` is the portal's front door and is now ours**: a cover carrying the mark and `Digital Portal` and nothing else, built from `css/portal.css` so it shares the portal's tokens, and needing no script. **White to the browser's edge** (2026-09-24, the user: "white background, together with the browser header"): `background: var(--card)`, `<meta name="theme-color" content="#ffffff">` and `color-scheme: light`, because a cover with no cards on it has nothing to lift off an off-white ground; the name is **15px, 500, in `--ink-soft`**, not the 22 to 30px display face ("smaller words, to be more minimalist and modern"), so the mark leads. No sign in and no menu of the rooms inside: every door into this portal is a link somebody was sent (the console behind Supabase Auth, the client pages behind their own tokens), so a list of them would be an index of what exists and a set of dead ends for anybody without a link. The user asked for it on 2026-09-20; the rule below still holds for every other root file.
- **`404.html` is the portal's too, from 2026-09-21**, at the user's request ("can you revamp the 404 to match the digital portal design system"): this host is the portal, so the page every wrong address on it lands on had no business reading as somewhere else. It is the cover this portal gives "Link not recognised" — the mark on a bar of `--head-h`, the title, the line that says what to do rather than restating the title, and two small controls (`.btn-sm`: Visit website, Contact support) in the documented order at their own widths on a desk and as equal halves on a phone. **English only** since 2026-09-24 (the user: "for page 404, show only in english"; it had printed the Chinese beneath), white to the browser's edge like the front door, the title 17px and the line 14px. Built from `css/portal.css`, so it carries the version stamp and moves with the tokens. Three things went with the old page: a `noscript` redirect to adspacestudios.com (there because that page could not draw itself without script; this one needs none, so the redirect would only have thrown a reader off a page they could already read), and `/css/main.css` and `/fonts/Custom.TTF`, neither of which exists on this host. It is in `uxaudit`'s walk beside the front door.
- Root HTML files (`ap01.html`, `ap02.html`, `ap03.html`, `ap-dale.html`, `accv-new.html`, `3pform.html`, `einvoiceinfo.html`, `interview-quiz.html`, `sales-program.html`, `supplier.html`) are the user's existing site. Never edit them.
- Portal pages are folders with an `index.html` and clean paths (`/admin/`, `/creators/`, `/review/`), never `.html` in a URL. New pages start from `docs/PAGE-TEMPLATE.html`.
- One stylesheet `css/portal.css`; one script per section in `js/`; `js/api.js` is the only Supabase client; `js/money.js` the only money formatter; `js/chrome.js` the only header and footer; `js/menu.js` the only copy of where a row ⋯ opens and what closes it; `js/copy.js` the only copy of what a Copy button says when it has copied (four buttons each said it differently: one swapped its label, one a span inside itself, one wrote into a `.msg` line, one tinted itself); **`js/words.js` the only copy of a word two pages share** (loaded before every page script); **`js/group.js` the only copy of a directory group** (the heading that folds, its count, the card with its header row and the Show more); **`js/sheet.js` the only copy of how a form sheet opens, shuts and refuses to throw typed work away**; **`js/form.js` the only copy of a segment and a More details fold** (a `select[data-seg]` drawn as a radio group, the select kept as the source of truth; a `details.fmore` whose line says what it holds); **`js/cmdbar.js` the only copy of what a command bar becomes on a phone** (the Filters button, its badge, the sheet the selects come up in, the ⋯ that holds a second action); **`js/state.js` the only copy of how a list says it is loading, has nothing in it, or could not be read** (three files had written those three states four different ways, and one of the four reported a failed read as an empty list). No build step, no framework, no bundler, ES5-style function scripts wrapped in an IIFE.
- **Server-side code that is not a Supabase edge function lives in `workers/`**, one folder per Worker, with its source and a README naming the account, the zone, the Worker, the custom domain and the three variables. `workers/links/` is the `hi.adspace.me` redirector. It is deployed from the repository and is not part of the Pages build.
- Docs for setup live in `docs/` (`CONTENT-REVIEW-SETUP.md`, `S3-UPLOAD-SETUP.md`, `S3-STORAGE.md`, `DRIVE-IMPORT-CHECK.md`, `FIX-ACCESS-DENIED.md`, `CREATOR-UPLOAD-CHECK.md`, `OPERATIONS-SETUP.md`, `OPERATIONS-MIGRATION.md`, `GOOGLE-MEET-SETUP.md`, `PERFORMANCE-SETUP.md`).
- **The portal writes to S3 and never removes anything** (`docs/S3-STORAGE.md`, 2026-09-21). An **orphan** — an object no row points at, left by a failed save or a deleted client — is invisible to the portal and billable for ever, and an abandoned multipart upload is billable and invisible even in the object list. The answer in order of value: **a lifecycle rule in two halves, applied at different times** (2026-09-21) — the housekeeping half now (abort incomplete uploads after 7 days, `NoncurrentVersionExpiration` at 30), because an abandoned 1 GB upload is billable storage that never appears in the object list and is a fifth of the Free Tier's 5 GB; the **storage-class half deliberately not yet**, because the Free Tier covers S3 **Standard only**, so on a small bucket a transition moves data out of what is free into what is billed and adds a per-request charge, a 30 day IA minimum and a per-GB retrieval fee to save a fraction of a cent per GB. Standard-IA at 90 and Glacier Instant Retrieval at 365 are added once the bill shows a storage line worth reducing, in practice past about 100 GB or when the Free Tier's twelve months end. **No expiration action at any size**, because a client asking for last year's reel and being told it is gone is worse than the bill. The first version of this doc recommended the transitions unconditionally, which was wrong at the volume this portal actually holds; **deleting an object when its row goes**, which is not built and needs a predicate that reads `campaign_deliverables.file_url`, `campaigns.invoice_url`, `clients.logo_url`, `drive_assets.url` and — the awkward one — **inside `posts.media`, a jsonb array of `{url, type, poster}`**, with a soft-removed deliverable counting as still in use because its Undo restores the row; and **splitting the IAM identity** so the portal's key may only `s3:PutObject` under `content/` and gains `s3:DeleteObject` only when that delete path ships. A key that cannot delete cannot be made to delete.
- **A change to one database function ships as a file in `supabase/migrations/`, not as a re-run of the whole schema.** `supabase/schema.sql` carries fifteen top-level data migrations (team roles and their defaults, the rate card minimums and inclusions, client slugs, the stage clock backfill, the team stand-down repair, the Marketing and rednote renames) and drops and recreates fifty-six policies, triggers and indexes. Every one of those is idempotent and several are guarded, but re-running all of it on a live database to replace one function body is unnecessary exposure. A migration file is dated, narrowly scoped, safe to run twice, carries its own rollback, and is applied standalone by `tests/sql.js`.
- Secrets never enter the repo. The Supabase anon key and the Google browser key are public by design and held back by their restrictions (Google key: websites `digital.adspace.me/*`, Drive API only). The delete code lives in the database.

### Infrastructure and assets (established, do not re-ask)
- Hosting: GitHub Pages, custom domain digital.adspace.me. Cloudflare Pages (`adspace-form.pages.dev`) only builds PR previews; the move to Cloudflare Pages was tried and abandoned (Access Denied), the site stays on GitHub Pages.
- Supabase project `hwwuigvdfubuymchsvyx`; tables and policies in `supabase/schema.sql`; `activity_viewers` is the older per-email list, superseded by user groups.
- Media: S3 bucket `myadspace`, region `ap-southeast-5`, prefix `content`, ARN `arn:aws:s3:::myadspace`, public read through CloudFront at https://mycdn.adspace.me; uploads by signed PUT from the `sign-upload` edge function; Supabase storage capped at 50 MB, so video goes to S3.
- The PDF is never stored: the portal keeps the data snapshot in `client_documents` (client, contact, lines, totals) and redraws the file on Download. Void keeps the row; Delete (only after Void) removes it and the letter can no longer be regenerated. Nothing goes to S3 or the CDN unless a person uploads it (the campaign invoice PDF is the one upload).
- Brand assets: header mark https://mycdn.adspace.me/adspace-brandname.png (`brandLogo`), favicon and og:image https://mycdn.adspace.me/adspace-favicon.png; in the repo for print: `css/adspace-mark.png` (the monogram, with alpha, taken from the reference letter), `css/SlateBook.TTF` (text), `css/SlateRg.TTF` (heavier face), `css/OPTIMA.TTF` (the wordmark). Older `ADspace.png` and the website favicon were rejected.
- Issuer (`ADSPACE_ORG` in `js/config.js`, entered by the user): ADSPACE PLT, registration 202304002162, SST J31-2401-32100002, 61-02, Jalan Mutiara Emas 2A, Taman Mount Austin, 81100 Johor Bahru, Johor, Malaysia, advertise@adspacestudios.com, 60187625233 (printed as (60)18 762 5233), adspacestudios.com. Bank line still blank.
- Emails: support and issuer advertise@adspacestudios.com, account manager marketing@adspacestudios.com, admin login adspacestudios@gmail.com. Terms https://adspacestudios.com/legal/policies.
- Short links: new links are written under `ADSPACE_CONFIG.linkHost` (`hi.adspace.me`); `go.adspace.me` stays alive for the Rebrandly QR codes already printed on slides, which encode the whole address. About 50 slugs on Rebrandly. No redirector is built for either host yet.
- Google Drive import uses the browser key in `js/config.js`; the Drive folder link is the only URL accepted (`/folders`).

### Behavioural guardrails the user gave (verbatim spirit)
- "This should be automatic on your structure when designing." Every rule in `DESIGN.md` is applied before the user sees a screenshot, never after.
- "Do not state the obvious." "Be concise and direct." No explanatory copy anywhere.
- "Return means go back." Back for navigation, Revert for a state, Restore for a record, Reinstate for a person.
- "Every action needs a way back." Nothing is deleted by one click; soft remove, Undo, Void then Delete.
- "Big card for short messages": a message is a line, not a card.
- "Same status throughout": the console and the client page use one vocabulary and one colour set.
- "Prefilled, not fixed": catalogue values seed a line and stay editable.
- "A phone screenshot is reviewed, not just taken."
- "Why do I need to fill in the handles here": intake asks for what is known on day one only.
- "Not AI SaaS": flat, quiet, corporate, Apple and Cloudflare register.
- "Memory like a goldfish": when a rule is learned it is written here the same push, with the section it belongs to, so it never has to be repeated.
- **Write to the user in corporate register: short, concise, straightforward, instructions first.** This governs the replies in the chat, not only the copy on the screen. No literary sentences, no narration of the reasoning, no essay where a list will do: what changed, what it means for them, what they have to do. Asked for several times before it was written down, which is the failure this line exists to stop.

# Part 2. STANDARD.md §24 Project Memory as of 2026-09-26

## 24. Project Memory

Update this section only with verified, durable facts. Keep entries short and remove stale or contradictory entries.

### Product decisions

- **2026-09-26 — The Cloudflare check sits under the row, once a card, in the page's colours.** Reported with two photos: on `/client/` the box joined the email and Send link row and squeezed the email to a sliver, drawn black on a light page; on `/admin/` Sign in and the passkey button each drew their own box. `js/captcha.js` now places the box after the row, shares one box a card (`data-cf`) and passes the page's theme; an autofilled field keeps `--card`. Nothing to run by hand. Verified by `signin: ok` (both pages, dark device, 1280 and 390).
- **2026-09-26 — Department, Position and Role standard live on the Team page; HR letters head a Team column; bars run to the edges; Show more is readable.** A colleague's department and role standard are set on their Team sheet (with Position, the old Designation) and Performance reads them from the team row; the review profile keeps only its two ticks. The row reads `ID · Department, Position`. The HR Letters card names the colleague from the Team directory, matched by the letter or by the Employee ID in its reference. The console's sign-in bar and every page's foot run to the screen edges. Show more's hover is no longer black. **To run by hand**: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-26-department-and-role-on-the-team.sql` (it moves the values once and splits "Creative, Production Executive"; its last statement lists the result). Verified by `perf: ok`, `perfui: ok`, `team: ok`, `docs: ok`, the sweep, `uxaudit: ok`, `matrix: ok`.
- **2026-09-26 — The sign-in page holds still; a deleted group stays deleted; Face ID named.** The header no longer moves on load (the mark's size kept from the last visit, the brand held until the mark lands on a first visit, the footer drawn with the header, the first paint white when signed out), and the phone mark is capped in `vw` so Safari cannot draw it 0px wide (not verifiable in the sandbox; the user confirms on the iPhone). The schema seeds Marketing and Sales only into an empty `team_roles`, so a re-run no longer puts a deleted group back, and the Team page names a refused group delete. The passkey offer reads Touch ID, Face ID or your device password. Nothing to run by hand; delete Sales again on the Team page. Verified by `levels: ok`, `team: ok`, the sweep, `uxaudit: ok`, `matrix: ok`.
- **2026-09-26 — Last activity, person in charge handover, passkeys, the Safari status bar.** Last activity is the newest of a call or visit, a stage move and a document issued, with the kind under the date (the user chose B). Standing a colleague down asks who takes their clients and open campaigns (Keep first), and a rename carries the new name onto their records, because `owner` is a name held as text. The console signs in with a passkey (supabase-js 2.117.2 on `/admin/` only), manages them from the account menu, and offers one once after an email sign-in. On touch screens the top bar is opaque so Safari's status bar takes its colour. The "Link not recognised" line now reads "Please contact your ADspace account manager." **To do by hand**: turn passkeys on in Supabase with RP ID `digital.adspace.me` and origin `https://digital.adspace.me` (`docs/SIGN-IN-SECURITY.md` §1a); redeploy the `hi.adspace.me` Worker for its new cover line (`workers/links/README.md`). Asked for by the user. Verified by `docs: ok`, `reports: ok`, `adsreport: ok`, `work: ok`.

Access is the section `reports` (View / Work / Manage), moved once from `clients.reports` or Clients, so a colleague can prepare reports without reading client records. The first kind is the Social Media Accounts Report. **To run by hand**: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-25-report-builder.sql` (after the social media reports migration). Verified by `reports: ok`, `adsreport: ok`, `smsql: ok` and the gate.
- **2026-09-25 — A format per task in Bulk add.** A month's tasks can be mixed formats: one format for every task, or Set each task with a row a task named by its week. `ops_generate_month` takes `formats` in creation order and refuses a list of the wrong length. **To run by hand**: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-25-a-format-per-task.sql`, after `2026-09-25-bulk-add-from-the-month.sql`. Verified by `ops: ok` §28 and `work: ok`.
- **2026-09-25 — Sign in with the emailed code; report figures formatted.** The /admin sign-in card takes the code from the email as well as the link, for the installed app. Report figure fields show thousands separators, percentages to one decimal and RM / S$ on money, and an ad's age split must add up to 100%. Nothing to run by hand. Verified by `signin: ok` and `adsreport: ok`.
- **2026-09-25 — The owner moves the task.** From the team's feedback: every task has an owner from creation (the creator by default), only the current owner or an admin moves its stage (`not-owner`), the step asks who takes a hand-off with Keep me as the Task Owner as the tick, Add task requires a client or Internal, and the task sheet folds files, time and activity under More. **To run by hand**: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-25-the-owner-moves-the-task.sql`, after `2026-09-25-bulk-add-from-the-month.sql`. Verified by `ops: ok` and `work: ok`.
- **2026-09-25 — Bulk add only into a confirmed month; both dates on the calendar; an admin moves a date.** Asked for by the user: Bulk add picks the client's own content months that are open with the meeting set (or marked not applicable) and prefills the month's plan; the database refuses any other month. The calendar shows each task's due date and publish date, the publish date in its own colour. An admin moves a due date without the approval round. Reports offer Active clients only. **To run by hand**: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-25-bulk-add-from-the-month.sql`. Verified by `ops: ok` and `work: ok`.
- **2026-09-25 — Social media advertising reports.** The second report type, from the team's first-month and later-month templates: a first month carries the reading guidance and no comparison, a later month compares against the previous period carried forward from the last advertising report. Ads are grouped by objective in the PDF, pasted from an Ads Manager export (an age breakdown gathered into one ad), and priced as Ads Manager prices them (a reach result per 1,000). **To run by hand**: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-25-report-builder.sql`, after the social media reports migration. Verified by `smsql: ok`, `adsreport: ok` and the gate.
- **2026-09-25 — Confirm creators is reachable from three places.** After the client confirms their picks the team still confirms them to lock the selection; that button sat only under the Selection list and was reported missing. The campaign's header line (`1 creator to confirm.`) now carries **Review and confirm**, which opens Selection on the button, and the Creators tab carries its own **Confirm N creators** under the bookings, pressing the same act. Nothing to run by hand. Verified by `camp: ok` and the gate.
- **2026-09-25 — Social media reports, published to the client after an internal confirmation.** Draft, Submit for review, Confirm (a manager who did not submit it), Publish to client; Revise and Unpublish after. The client reads only a frozen published version. Each PDF section starts its own page on a golden-ratio scale with a 33.3pt margin. **To run by hand**: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-25-social-media-reports.sql`. Verified by `smsql: ok`, `reports: ok` and the gate.
- **2026-09-25 — Stage dates are editable; Complete is a tick.** Every Timeline stage date can be corrected, Client since is the first stage's start, and the order is enforced on the page; the My Work report uses formal headings with more space; the iOS tap highlight is off. Asked for by the user from screenshots. Nothing to run by hand. Verified by `crm: ok`, `work: ok` and the gate.
- **2026-09-25 — The console installs as an app.** `/admin/` only: a manifest, wordmark icons, and a service worker that serves an offline cover when the network is gone and caches nothing else, so a release still reaches everybody on their next load. Asked for by the user (first raised 2026-09-14, set aside, raised again 2026-09-25). Nothing to run by hand. Verified by `pwa: ok` and the sweep.
- **2026-09-25 — The desk search is a mark on every bar.** Every console command bar's search is My Work's mark that grows into a 280px field, at a desk as on a phone; supersedes "the desk bar is unchanged" of 2026-09-22 for the search only. Asked for by the user. Verified by `cmdbar: ok` and the gate.
- **2026-09-25 — The user group panel, revamped.** A live summary sentence at the top, Start from Admin / Manager / Staff / View only (Custom when the panel matches none), each section a segment with one line saying what its level allows, and the parts under Fine-tune (n) counting only exceptions. The stored access map and its rules are unchanged; the preset is derived from the panel, never stored. No preset below Admin opens Team, HR letters or performance reviews. Nothing to run by hand. Verified by `perms: ok`, `team: ok`, `levels: ok`, the sweep, `uxaudit: ok`, `matrix: ok`.
- **2026-09-24 — Reconciled after #181.** The email code is mandatory for every member (supersedes the per-person switch; run `supabase/migrations/2026-09-24-performance-code-always.sql` after the email-code migration). The Client scope takes past clients on request (`-tasks-for-past-clients.sql`). My Work's view segment leads the desk bar and search is a mark; an open ⋯ follows its button on resize; List, Board and Calendar are their own view permissions; Team → Performance starts at June 2026 with a padlock beside the tab; sheet cards are spaced 16px; vague headings replaced. The permissions panel revamp is a proposal for the user's decision, not built. Verified by the sweep, `ops: ok`, `perf: ok`, `sql: ok`, `uxaudit: ok`, `matrix: ok`.
- **2026-09-24 — My performance behind an email code.** A member may turn on a lock on their own reviews: a 6-digit code emailed to them, good for 15 minutes, checked by the database from the signed token's `amr` claim; turning it off needs a code too. Decided by the user: each person for themselves, a code rather than a link. **To run by hand**: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-24-performance-email-code.sql` after the performance migration, and add `{{ .Token }}` to the Supabase Magic Link email template. Verified by `perf: ok` and `perfguard: ok`.
- **2026-09-24 — A creator keeps their own profile links.** The creator portal opens on a head card (name, since when, bookings, profile links) and the creator edits their links with no approval round; the safeguards are the database's link reader, the refusal of a profile another creator holds, and every change kept with the set it replaced, restorable from Link history on the Creators List. The fee is never on the page. **To run by hand**: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-24-creator-profile.sql`. Verified by `cprofile: ok`, `sql: ok`, the affected suites, `uxaudit: ok`.
- **2026-09-24 — Segments slide; the permission panel says each level once.** The chosen option of every segment is one surface that slides under a new choice (`ADspaceForm.thumb()`), and My Work draws a view a frame after the press. In the user group panel a granted part offers No access once and only the levels the database checks (`PART_LEVELS`, restated from `ops_granted()` / `allowed()`), a level stored before that is shown and saved as what it grants, and parts are rows on one right edge. Reported by the user with screenshots. Nothing to run by hand. Verified by `slide: ok`, `perms: ok`, the sweep, `uxaudit: ok`, `matrix: ok`.
- **2026-09-24 — The form system, site-wide.** Secondary buttons are tonal grey (`--tonal`, never an outline, which is the fields' shape); every create and edit sheet is titled sections of two to five fields, a segment for a choice of two to four (`select[data-seg]`, `js/form.js`, the select kept as the source of truth), and a More details fold for the defaults whose line says what they hold; Billing and Brand read first and edit in a sheet. Decided by the user from the form audit ("Proceed all three"). Nothing to run by hand. Verified by the sweep, `geom: ok`, `uxaudit: ok`, `matrix: ok`.
- **2026-09-24 — One split per form; Cmd + Enter.** Rows of fields use `.fgrid` equal columns, never per-field pixel widths (the Billing pane split at three different points); measured by `tests/geom.js`. Cmd/Ctrl + Enter presses the nearest main action and never a destructive one (`tests/keys.js`). **Decided the same day and built in the next batch** (below).
- **2026-09-24 — Performance reviews, phase 1.** The Performance Framework v2.0 is a record in the portal: management scores a month, logs breaches, releases it at the 1-1; the member reads only their own released months (My performance), may dispute for 3 days item by item, acknowledges; management answers and finalises, and prints the monthly record. Decisions: grade names Distinction / Strong / Baseline / Needs Guidance / Performance Review; C is eligible unless the previous month was C; L3/L4 breach makes a month not eligible; pacing only for people who run ads; breaches hidden until release. **Two locks for management**: `team.performance` (granted in the user group, never inherited) and the master code, set by hand with `select public.perf_code_reset('…')` in the SQL editor, never in the repository. **Phase 2 (not built)**: quarterly and company rewards (revenue gate RM 500,000) and growth commission. **To run by hand**: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-24-performance-reviews.sql`, then set the code. Verified by `perf: ok` and `perfui: ok`.
- **2026-09-24 — Bulk add spreads the month.** `ops_generate_month` shares a count across weeks 1 to 4 (task i in week floor(i·4/n)+1), dates each task inside its own week, and never rewrites a code already made. **To run by hand**: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-24-bulk-add-spreads-the-month.sql`. Verified by `ops: ok` §25 and `work: ok`.
- **2026-09-24 — Sheets step 16px; root pages white.** Every field in a sheet sits 16px under the one above (one rule, no per-row style), measured by `tests/geom.js`. `/` and `/404` are white to the browser's edge (`theme-color` white), the front door's name is 15px, and the 404 is English only.
- **2026-09-24 — Readiness is the client's first month.** The Onboarding and Pre-advertising ticks are seeded only on a client's first month; later months go Ready on the meeting alone, existing later-month ticks fold into the first month, and deleting a first month hands its ticks on. **One migration to run by hand**, after `2026-09-24-google-meet.sql`: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-24-readiness-is-the-first-month.sql`. `meet-create` now names a missing secret and Google's refusal reason; **the function is redeployed by hand** after this merge. Verified by `ops: ok` §24 and `work: ok` §20. **Later the same day**: the first live booking worked and its link was missing from the message, because Google answered before the Meet existed; `meet-create` now waits for the link and records a slow one as pending rather than booking twice. **Redeploy `meet-create` by hand after each change to it.**
- **2026-09-24 — The content meeting on Google Meet.** The meeting is booked on the shared ADspace calendar (adspacestudios@gmail.com) by the `meet-create` edge function, whose refresh token lives in its Supabase secrets and never in the repository or a browser; a slot any other event on that calendar holds is refused and named. Until the secrets are set the portal says Google Meet is not connected and everything else works, pasted links included. The message to the client is the team's bilingual template filled from the month (`clientMessage()`, the only copy). A comment now notifies the task's owner. **To run by hand, in order**: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-24-google-meet.sql` after `2026-09-24-the-month-in-two-ticks.sql`; then `docs/GOOGLE-MEET-SETUP.md` (Calendar API, consent screen published, refresh token, three secrets, `supabase functions deploy meet-create` with Verify JWT off). Verified by `ops: ok` and `work: ok` §20; the live Google call is not exercised by any suite, because the sandbox cannot reach Google and no token exists yet.
- **2026-09-24 — The month in two ticks, and a phone that looks like the desk.** From the user's review of the live portal: readiness is two ticks (Onboarding checklist, Pre-advertising checklist) with who ticked stamped by the database; the month's ticks, meeting and Mark planning complete are acted on from the task's next step; a content deliverable made from My Work joins or makes its client's month; a month is deleted from its card with a reason (ops Manage). **One migration to run by hand**, after `2026-09-24-my-work-daily-tasks.sql`: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-24-the-month-in-two-ticks.sql` (mirrored in `supabase/schema.sql`, compared by `tests/ops.js`). **The phone's 16px field floor is replaced**: `js/chrome.js` adds `maximum-scale=1` on iOS alone (focus zoom stopped, pinch kept, WCAG 1.4.4 met), and phone controls take the desk's scale a step up (fields 15px in 44px, small controls 14px in 38px, status selects 13.5px). A missing database function now reads "This needs a database update" rather than PostgREST's schema-cache message, which the user hit because the daily-task migration had not been run. The bell is read every minute while the tab is on screen and whenever somebody returns to it; it was read once at sign-in. Nobody is told about their own act, so a console worked by one shared account never rings — by design; comment notifications followed the same day, with the Google Meet batch.
- **2026-09-24 — My Work is the team's daily task list, merged on the user's green light.** Built from the user's briefs "Reframe My Work as lightweight daily task tracking" and "Correct the My Work task structure", their SOP of 2026-09-23 and decisions A to G ("1 proceed all A to G / 2 work with a reason (published) / 3 open and merge after patch"), then patched from their review while they were away ("create pr merge and push under green light"). The decisions: **A** a content task's row shows its real stage and a stage select; **B** the published filters are back (In progress, Due soon, AQC review, Client review, Waiting on client, Late); **C** Group by stage, keeping status; **D** the head's meta does not repeat the format; **E/F** the words (Task Owner, Created by, Bulk add, `#WT00001`); **G** skipping a step is ops Work with a reason, as published, superseding the earlier tightening to Manage. The structure: the list stays and a task opens in the portal's own side sheet of layered cards, the full record one press further; blue only for a hand-off; the Task Owner changed on the step by an unticked tick; the timer off the default path. Added from the review: **the draft link is optional** ("we have a WhatsApp group"): the step reads Sent on WhatsApp and records it, and the database takes the link or a note; **every added thing has edit, delete and Undo, and every stage a Revert** ("check if all comes with edit / delete / revert"); the card actions follow the design system (a quiet action per card opening a small form, one filled action at a time). **Two migrations to run by hand, in this order**: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-24-my-work-daily-tasks.sql` (mirrored in `supabase/schema.sql`, statements and header compared by `tests/ops.js`), then `supabase/migrations/2026-09-24-clear-test-tasks.sql`, once and on purpose, because it deletes the test tasks and resets the numbering. Found in the final pass and fixed: a cancelled task could not come back although the cancel sheet said it could (Reopen now on every cancelled task, and `ops_transition_task` clears `cancelled_at` when a task leaves Cancelled); Escape shut the whole sheet under an open ⋯; the step card's Assign task owner and Set due date did nothing in the sheet, because they opened the full record's controls; the In progress view missed stages whose group is the work but whose flag was unset. And the SOP's stage names were cut off in the 124px state column ("Performance r", "Revision (Inter"): the task row's stage track is 160px and every state select ends a longer name in an ellipsis; on a narrow list the stage jumped between lines by the words in the row, and now keeps one place; between 640 and 860 the list's task name was squeezed to 64 to 108px, and the list now states its own narrow line (`data-narrow`, read by `ADspaceState.fit`). Known limit: a Revert back into a review counts as a new round, because the round is counted off the moves into that stage. Verified by `ops: ok`, `sql: ok`, `work: ok`, the sweep, `matrix: ok` and `uxaudit: ok`.
- **2026-09-23 — Operations phase 4, merged to `main` as #170.** The user's brief "Digital Portal: My Work and operational workflow changes" (eighteen sections) built on its own branch and PR at their instruction ("Do not auto-merge or deploy until migrations, workflows and responsive layouts have been reviewed"). The decisions taken with them: **D1** the term adjustment stays a tick and gains a percentage prefilled from the rate card and editable (`client_services.term_pct`); **D2** the publish date is the *scheduled* publish date, tentative until the content meeting and never required, and never rewrites the name; **D3** the code's month is the content month; **D4** the `T1xxx` serial stays the identity and the name is `YYMMW{week}{seq} description`; **D5** tasks from before get no code; **D6** a hand-over on a stage move is Work level, a plain reassignment stays Manage; **D7** Client scope is stage `active` by default with paused on request, Lead is lead/contacted/proposal; **D8** the General and Video workflows are retired for new tasks; **D9** the edge-to-edge bar and foot are the client-facing pages' and not the console's. Two migrations to run by hand, in this order: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-23-operations-phase4.sql` (the data model, the content workflow, the functions; mirrored in `supabase/schema.sql` and compared byte for byte by `tests/ops.js`) and `supabase/migrations/2026-09-23-price-list.sql`, after its `-preview.sql`. Verified by `ops: ok`, `sql: ok`, `work: ok`, `crm: ok`, the sweep, `matrix: ok` and `uxaudit: ok`.
- **2026-09-23** — The engagement (one client's work for one month) lives on the client record's **Work** pane and is drawn by `js/ops.js` (`ADspaceOps.clientWork`), never by a second copy in `js/crm.js`. Ready and In production on it are the database's to grant (every check Ready or Not applicable, the meeting held or marked not applicable), and a task's move into production waits on the same two facts. Nobody is told by the portal when a month becomes ready; the checklist and the meeting line are what the team reads.
- **2026-09-23** — The rate card v2.0.5 changes wording and adds two lines, and moves no price; `supabase/migrations/2026-09-23-price-list.sql` is guarded line by line on the seed's value, so a correction the team made on the Services page is left alone, and its preview file says per line what will happen before anything is written.
- **2026-09-15** — The portal is an internal operations console plus three client-facing pages, not a public product. Sections and their rules are enumerated in `CLAUDE.md`; the design system and the reasoning behind each rule are in `DESIGN.md`. Both are imported by `CLAUDE.md` and take priority 3 under §2 of this file.
- **2026-09-15** — Creators sign in with an eight character code rather than an account or an SMS one time code. Reason: the agency holds phone numbers rather than addresses, SMS costs money on every message, and a freelancer has no password to remember. The code travels in the link and is stored by the browser.
- **2026-09-15** — A creator is shown their own booking only. The client's stage, the campaign's commercial state, the client's amount, other creators, and the team's notes are withheld by the security definer function, never only by the page.
- **2026-09-15 — approved, NOT YET BUILT.** A creator's submission goes to the team before it goes to the client. `creator_submit` currently sets the step straight to `reviewing`, which on the client's page reads "Your approval": the chip asks the client to act the moment the creator uploads, while `campaign_deliverables` is team-only, so there is nothing there for them to open and the buttons only draw when somebody has separately pasted a `draft_url`. A **Submitted** step goes between `pending_draft` and `reviewing`. The client reads Submitted as "Draft in progress", because nothing has reached them. The team then either **releases to the client**, which is what exposes the creator's uploaded file URLs to the client-facing function so the client opens the creator's own files and the Drive folder leaves the loop, or sends it back to the creator as Changes requested with a note — an internal round the client never sees. Decided with the user on 2026-09-15; its own branch and PR, after the upload fix merges.

- **2026-09-16, reversed 2026-09-22** — The Clients directory was one Client Register with the stages as divider rows inside it. The user sent the divider rows back (the uppercase eyebrow and its spacing) and asked for a card per stage, as the rate card draws Services and Add-ons; the Register follows the same shape, a card per family. Each card carries its own header row and a 15px heading with the count and marks; Past clients stays shut by default.
- **2026-09-16** — The client record's Overview is a summary composed only from what the record has already loaded (contacts, the billing ring, service lines, touches, documents). No second read, no stored number, no invented metric. Reason: the pane somebody lands on had less on it than any other, and a metric nobody stores is a metric that goes stale silently.

- **2026-09-16** — The client record opens on an identity area (mark, name, state, and the facts that identify rather than describe) and its Overview is the record itself: flat titled sections over the contacts, services, letters and calls the record has already read. The rail is one block per question and a block leaves when its data does not exist. Reason: the pane somebody lands on had less on it than any other, and a rail of headings over "Not set" is a rail nobody reads.
- **2026-09-16** — Billing and Brand are already dedicated panes, so their forms are not hidden inside a second disclosure card. Imported clients may correct **Client since** from Key dates; the time in the current stage is the stage clock, stated once in the rail's Account status block and never as a second duration field.
- **2026-09-21** — The record head is two parts: who this is on the left (mark, name with its stage, meta), one ⋯ on the right holding Edit and Delete. Edit is no longer a standing control beside the stage, on the client record or the campaign record. The rail opens on Account status (stage chip plus stage clock), its titles are the pane's 15px section size, the billing gate is red and opens Billing, and date and activity rows carry neutral glyphs. Decided with the user on 2026-09-21 against a reference design; the reference's `Add contact` in the head was not taken, because the Contacts pane already carries that action as its one blue primary and a second copy in the head would be a second primary on every pane.
- **2026-09-22** — The state lives in the record head's right part beside the ⋯ on both records: the campaign's chip and the client's stage select. The left part is the mark, the name and the meta only. On a phone (below 1100) the client rail splits around the panes: Account status, Next action, the billing gate and Profile stay above the tab strip, and Key dates, Details and Recent activity follow the pane (`.rail-after`, `display: contents` on the rail, `order` on the blocks). Recent activity is last by decision: it is an excerpt of the Activity pane, read after the record. The client portal's rail is excluded from the split because its rows key on the rail's own width class.
- **2026-09-21** — The client record's **Key dates block is the Timeline**, and it is the one place every date and every duration lives. The stage journey is a row per stage (when it began, how long it ran) above a rule, the dates the record holds are below it, and the stage that is running carries the weight and the overdue mark. **The Account status block is gone**, superseding the 2026-09-21 decision that the rail opens on it: with the clock moved here, the block repeated the stage select the head already carries. The journey's mute line under Details is gone for the same reason. Each stage row is a turnaround the team asked to be able to average later (speed to first contact, contact to proposal, proposal to close, how long engaged) and **nothing new is stored for any of it** — `stage_log` has been stamped since the clock shipped, so a report across every client needs no field and no backfill. **Client since is edited in place**: the value becomes the picker and the pen beside it becomes the tick (`ADspaceAsk.rename`, extended once with `type` and `value` so a date can seed its field differently from the words on the row); the separate field and Save button are gone. Asked for by the user on 2026-09-21 ("client since ... the date could be clickable and editable so we don't need another date button", "all those dates days related could fall under key dates? or maybe timeline"). Verified by `node tests/crm.js tests` → `crm: ok`, `uxaudit: ok`, `matrix: ok`.
- **2026-09-22** — A revamp mockup (flat edge-to-edge registers for Services and Team) was reviewed against the documented rules. Taken: one Price heading over the amount and the unit on the rate card, counts on its category bands, a neutral `You` chip on the member's own row. Declined, each by a rule already recorded here: a page title with a blurb (explanatory copy; the bar names the route), a Status column with a dot and Active on every row (the accent marks the exception), the group as a column on every member (headings, decided 2026-09-16), and an access summary per member (`Clients + 4 more` hides four of five facts and repeats the group's access per person). The flat register without a bounded panel is a portal-wide decision across every register and the record panes, not a page at a time, and has not been made.
- **2026-09-22** — Each console route carries a one line purpose under its command bar while the team is new to the portal, on the retiring `?` pattern (three views, then behind the mark). Decided by the user against the no-explanatory-copy rule because the portal is opening to the whole team; it is an instruction, so it retires, and standing facts (the verify page, the redirect host) are `.routenote` lines instead. The Team panel folds each section's parts under its own head, taken from the Bukku permissions screen the user showed; Bukku's level codes and per-state scope ticks were not taken. **Own clients only** (a row scope for Sales under Clients) was raised and is its own batch, not built.
- **2026-09-19** — The operations system's Phase 1 (the data model, the read rules and the server functions) is in `supabase/migrations/2026-09-19-operations-system.sql`, mirrored byte for byte in `supabase/schema.sql`. **Its permissions are the existing access ladder**, not the seven booleans the brief named: `ops` View/Work/Manage for use, create and assign, and `ops.all`, `ops.reports`, `ops.workflows`, `ops.time` for the team queue, reports, templates and another person's hours. **The four parts are granted, never inherited** (`ops_granted()`), because each widens what the section opens rather than narrowing it, and an inherited widening makes the safe configuration the one somebody has to remember. Decided against a parallel boolean system on the ground that two authorisation systems is a data-integrity defect (§2 priority 1), and reported to the user with the mapping.
- **2026-09-19** — Operations writes are security-definer functions only; the `ops_` tables carry a select policy and nothing else, so a browser cannot set a stage, a date, an assignment, a completion or an event. The one direct write is marking your own notification read. `expected_version` refuses a stale write with the current row. Original commitments are written once. Cycle time, stage time and active work are three separate measures and are never summed. Verified by `node tests/ops.js tests` → `ops: ok`.
- **2026-09-19** — My Work is built for about 290 tasks a month (eight contents a week across thirty clients), so: **the read is bounded and open work is not part of the bound** (open work in full however old, finished work from a chosen period, the period select drawn only while finished work can be on the page); **Group by** re-bands the same rows by due date, client, stage or owner, with every card shut off the due axis so the page is headings and counts, the heading carrying the overdue count a shut card would hide; the fold is remembered per axis (`ADspaceGroup.section`'s new `memo`, which separates a card's identity from what its fold is filed under); and **a stage moves from the row** through the same function and gates, which is why the row is no longer a single button — the name cell opens the record instead. Three global corrections went with it: `.rectabs` no longer bleeds past the page gutter on a phone, the step under a tab strip has one owner (the strip, at 12px, as `.tabrow` already did), and the `?` no longer inherits the action's `margin-left: auto`. Decided with the user on 2026-09-19 after they raised the volume; a kanban board was weighed and deferred to phase 3, scoped to in-flight stages, because a board of 290 cards is worse than a table.
- **2026-09-22** — Operations phase 3: My Work has three views of one set of rows (List, Board per workflow with WIP counts against `wip_guidance` and an On hold column, Calendar of final due dates), `view=` in the address; a capacity strip on the board reads this week's sessions against `team_members.capacity_minutes_week`, typed as hours on the Team panel; notifications are written by the database in `ops_log` (phase 3 migration: `ops_notify`, `ops_log` redefined) under one rule (the owner is told about another person's change, the new owner is told they were assigned, nobody is told about their own act, a dedupe key collapses a repeat inside a minute) and read by a bell in the console head. No new table, column, policy or permission. Phase 4 remains the client record's Operations tab, Content Review integration and recurring rules on screen; the report shipped on 2026-09-21 (below).
- **2026-09-21** — The operations report is a **fourth view** in My Work's segment (`view=report`), not a ninth route: the segment already means "the same rows asked a different question", and the rail is two chunks of four. One function, `ops_report(p_from, p_to)` (`supabase/migrations/2026-09-21-operations-report.sql`, mirrored in `supabase/schema.sql`), returns five answers — what is running, what is late, how long each stage takes, whether the work reached client review on time, and the same per person. It adds **no table, column, policy or permission**: every figure is derived from `ops_tasks`, `ops_workflow_stages` and the append-only `ops_task_events`, so there is nothing to backfill. `ops.reports` governs it and is granted, never inherited; the button is hidden where it is not held and `view=report` in the address falls back to the list, because a link somebody was sent must not open a view the database would deny. **Median with a 90th percentile and a count, never a bare figure** — one task stuck in Editing for three months moves a mean enough to say nothing about the ordinary case. **Replanning is counted beside the on-time rate and never folded into it**, or an extension erases the miss it was granted for. By person is the foundation of a KPI and not a KPI: no score, no ranking. Asked for by the user on 2026-09-21 ("a dashboard for admin / manager to see whats running, whats pending, whats on shooting … during performance meeting"). Verified by `node tests/ops.js tests` → `ops: ok` (the arithmetic against real Postgres) and `node tests/work.js tests` → `work: ok` (the view, its controls and the address).
- **2026-09-22** — The task record states each fact once: the number as a token heading the record (no `.rec-mark` disc; `.rec-id-plain`), the dates in the rail only (with `moved from` and `Move a date` there), the owner on the identity line and in the select that changes it (no read-only People row), and the number not repeated under Details. The rail's forward move is a button at its own width, never full width, and the rail does not repeat the head's stage chip. The owner control answers with `Saved.` and puts the select back on a refusal. The Overview is Brief, Video, Progress and Links; the rail gains Recent activity. Sent back by the user on 2026-09-22 ("the serial # is showing ugly", "change owner when saved nothing moved", "buttons no need use blue all the way") against reference designs whose cleanliness, not their layout, was the standard asked for. `tests/stub2.js` refuses any function by name through `window.__refuseRpc`.
- **2026-09-19** — Operations phase 2 is **My Work** (`js/ops.js`, `?s=work`), plus four functions in `supabase/migrations/2026-09-19-operations-phase2.sql` that phase 1's gates needed and had no control for: `ops_add_link`, `ops_set_link_archived`, `ops_set_checklist`, `ops_set_video`. The queue is banded by when the work is owed and drawn by `js/group.js`; the task is the `.rec` workspace with five panes and a rail. **The route's name and its permission key differ on purpose**: My Work on the screen and `?s=work` in the address, `ops` in the ladder, mapped once in `sectionAllowed()`. **Which stage move is "forward" is read from the workflow** (`forwardOf()`: nearest ahead by `position`, skipping the stage groups beside the main line), never from a list in the page: a hand-written preference put Ready in front of Internal review, and "not waiting" then put Revision in front of Client review, because a review stage waits on a reviewer and is still on the main line. **Whose queue you are looking at is a view, not a filter**: Clear the filters leaves the scope alone, and the scope select is offered only where `ops.all` is granted. **The count is read against the view somebody chose**, so the route's own default never prints a fraction. Verified by `node tests/work.js tests` → `work: ok`.
- **2026-09-22** — Every console directory is a card per group drawn by one component (`js/group.js`): Clients by stage, Content Review one card, Campaigns by state, the Creators List by fee band, Short Links Live and Paused, Documents by family, Services a card per category, Team a card per user group. Folds rather than tabs on a long directory, a card holding everything never shut by default, the card animating open and shut in place. The Register is named **Documents** in the nav (address and identifiers unchanged). A global console search is deferred until the lists are in the hundreds; the section search filters in place. Decided with the user on 2026-09-22.
- **2026-09-22** — A portal document is corrected by Reissue (`document_reissue`, Work level): the earlier version is voided as Reissued and kept, the new version keeps the same serial and points back at it (`documents.replaces`), the unique index is over standing documents only, and `serial_taken()` still refuses the serial to anything else. The verify page answers the standing version as Valid and never says reissued; the Register names the replaced version `Reissued`. Decided with the user on 2026-09-22 ("when verify do not show the status as reissue").
- **2026-09-22** — The Documents Register: one engine (`js/documents.js` pen, `js/letters.js` letters) for the quotation cover, the client letters and the HR letters, one table (`documents`) holding the snapshot, the file redrawn on Download and never stored. The quotation cover stays separate from the Letter of Offer and takes the accounting portal's reference typed; a client letter is `AD/[SA/]{client_code}/{code}`; an HR letter is `ADHR/{staff_code}/{code}{YYMM}`. HR letters are their own section of the access ladder (`hr`) beside `register`. The public `/verify/` page answers an exact reference with the kind, the date and Valid / Void / Replaced, never the recipient, and replaces the Jotform verification form; the root `verify.html` moved to `drafts/verify-jotform.html` on 2026-09-18 (excluded from the published site), so `/verify` and `/verify/` reach the new page. Decided with the user on 2026-09-22 from the four Word templates and five sample letters. **Storage on S3 with expiring public links, renewable and revocable from the console, is batch 2 and not built.** The ALP checklists are forms, not letters, and are a later batch.
- **2026-09-22** — `css/SlateMedium.TTF` (uploaded by the user) is named by `ADSPACE_ORG.fontMed` and heads the letters; the engine falls back to Slate Regular wherever the file cannot be fetched. The Chinese block of a quotation cover embeds Noto Sans TC from jsDelivr (`ADSPACE_ORG.fontCjk`), because Slate carries no Chinese; a letter with a Chinese block is refused by name when that face cannot be fetched. **2026-09-18**: a CFF based Chinese face is embedded whole, never subset, because pdf-lib 1.17.1 subsets a CID-keyed CFF font with the glyph order wrong and the first live cover printed ASCII glyphs for its Chinese; a cover with a Chinese block is a few megabytes for it until a TrueType face replaces the OTF.
- **2026-09-21** — `--warn` is `#a94d0c` (orange, 5.05:1 on its tint), no longer the brown `#9c5c16`. A blocking condition (the billing gate) is `--err`, not `--warn`: amber is caution, red is refusal or destruction.
- **2026-09-21** — Golden ratio, measured on the client record at 1440 and 1280: pane to rail 1.618 exactly; name 19px to meta 12px 1.58; contact label track 150px of a 652px row (0.23, the short side of a golden cut is 0.38, so labels are deliberately quieter than that); section title 15px to body 13.5px 1.11, which is the console's tight operational scale and is not brought to φ on purpose, per §9 (a φ step from 14px body puts section titles at 22px in a tool read all day). Spacing stays on 4/8/12/16/24/32.
- **2026-09-16** — A campaign schedule is an editable operational view ordered as Upcoming, then Past. It changes dates on the booking but never changes `campaign_options.position`; the client-facing creator sequence remains the sequence originally offered and selected. Time is a native optional time value, not free text.
- **2026-09-16** — A creator submission is due seven calendar days after a shoot by default. `campaign_options.submission_due` remains directly editable for delivery, remote and other no-visit work; the creator sees the date and a due/overdue countdown. Changing a shoot date updates its default deadline without changing the client-facing creator order.
- **2026-09-16** — A released client draft is reviewed in its booking card: portrait video uses a 9:16 inline player with native play, pause and fullscreen controls, and the caption sits with it. The decision sheet contains decisions only and closes with one X, not an X plus Cancel.
- **2026-09-16** — Client and campaign Activity panes use one three-column audit row: timestamp, event with its detail, and actor with a monogram. At narrow component widths the event owns the first line and date plus actor share the second. Activity never inherits the generic service-row phone grid; that collision stacked every cell at the same coordinates.

- **2026-09-16** — A letter's PDF is never stored and there is no signed upload: `client_documents` holds the snapshot and the file is redrawn on Download. A permanent deletion therefore has **no storage side, no object queue and no quarantine** — the row is the letter — and the console says the deletion is immediate and irreversible. Decided with the user on 2026-09-16 after the alternative (building a signed-letter upload first) was weighed and deferred.
- **2026-09-16** — The letter's closing carries the issuer's **name only**; no designation field was added. The guard against an internal role reaching a client's letterhead is therefore a refusal at issue time (`issuer_name_ok`), not a second field. Decided with the user on 2026-09-16.
- **2026-09-16** — Voiding a letter applies to a **verified** letter only and has no restore. An issued or signed letter has confirmed nothing, so there is nothing to reverse; removing one is a deletion. This narrows what the old `letter_set_void` accepted, and is a deliberate capability change rather than a regression.

- **2026-09-22** — Three reports of "the owner does not save" on a task were one ambiguous PostgREST embed: `ops_task_assignees` and `ops_work_sessions` each carry two foreign keys to `team_members`, so `team_members(name)` was refused (PGRST201) and every owner in My Work was blank, Mine matched nothing, and the record's select fell back to Nobody after a save that had worked. The foreign key is named in the hint; a refused read of the assignments now fails the list and the record rather than drawing an unowned task; `tests/stub2.js` refuses a bare ambiguous embed (`TWO_WAYS`) and now honours `.order()`, which it had been discarding — so "the last event" was the oldest one and the page and the stand-in agreed on the wrong answer.
- **2026-09-22** — My Work: the stage select is toned by `stage_group` in four families (mute / no paint / warn / green, with blocked red), a move from a row says so under that row, and every forward move has a **Revert** beside it whose target is read off the last stage change in the task's own events. `ops_delete_task` (its own dated migration, mirrored in `supabase/schema.sql` and compared by `tests/ops.js`) is the first removal of a task: **ops** Manage, the number typed back, a reason, no restore, and an `ops.deleted` row in the activity record because the task's own events cascade with it. Complexity is on the new-task sheet; the date sheet's reason categories are the team's own (Client request, Scope change, Internal capacity, Pending assets, Pending confirmation, Incorrect date listed). **Who approves an extension is not decided**: the move is recorded and the owner is notified, and an approval round would be its own change.
- **2026-09-22** — Copy: a destructive control is named **Delete** and never `Delete {noun}`, because the menu it sits in has already named the noun; the consequence is stated in the sheet that asks. `.kmenu-item.is-danger` is red on the item, not only on a bold child inside it.
- **2026-09-22** — The route's purpose line is a panel from the section's name (`.aboutpop`), not a block under the command bar: from inside a record the bar is off the screen, so the control that explains the section did nothing. It opens only when pressed — a panel that opens itself lands on the command bar and on a phone intercepts the press — and while a route is new the title's glyph carries `--action` instead. One sentence a route.
- **2026-09-22** — `.rectabs` is `flex: 0 0 auto`. As a scroll container its automatic minimum size is 0, so inside the Activity sheet's fixed-height flex card the section tabs were squeezed to no height and drew nothing, a regression from the `.acttabs` pill row they replaced.
- **2026-09-22** — The Activity record's access is a part per tab (`activity.clients`, `activity.ops`, `activity.team`, `activity.review`, `activity.campaigns`, `activity.links`, `activity.register`, `activity.services`), so one section's log can be opened to the team while the rest stays gated. The read policy asks `allowed('activity.' || activity_section(action), 'view')`; `activity_section()` restates the tag-to-section map that lives in `js/admin.js`'s `ACTION_LABEL`, because about thirty of its entries do not follow their tag's prefix and it cannot be derived. `tests/sql.js` §21 reads both copies and fails on any difference, and drives the policy for a group with the section, a group with one part and a group with neither against real Postgres. A tag nothing names answers `other`, which has no part, so it falls back to the section and fails open to whoever can read the record rather than hidden from everybody.

- **2026-09-22** — The console draws its own shell while `me()` is in flight (`.console.is-booting`: the rail and the bar present, their contents withheld, the body a skeleton). What stood there was the shared page bar over an empty page, which is the client-facing composition, so a refresh read as somebody else's portal. Withholding what a person may open and withholding the shape of the page are two different things.
- **2026-09-21** — Two dates, two authorities (`supabase/migrations/2026-09-21-draft-date-is-the-teams-own.sql`). The **first draft** date is the team's own milestone, freely adjustable by anybody who may work the task and gated by nothing: `ops_due_decider(p_task, p_kind)` answers null for `first_draft`. This closes the item open since 2026-09-20, where both memory files recorded an intent the shipped function contradicted and `tests/ops.js` asserted the code while flagging the difference. The **final** date keeps the approval round back to the task's creator, confirmed by the user on 2026-09-21 ("if changes needed it requires the approval flow back to the original created owner"). `ops_due_order_ok()` refuses a draft on or after the final date, compared by calendar day, inside `ops_change_due_date` as well as the asking path, because `ops_decide_due_change` reaches the move directly. The page draws two outlined controls, one per date, and the word **Move** is gone from both and from the sheet's title. The stand-in read `p_when` where the page sends `p_value`, so every date move in a test wrote `undefined` and reported success.
- **2026-09-21 — stated, NOT YET BUILT.** The final due date is the day the work is owed **at Client review**, not a square on a calendar. A task whose final date has passed while its stage is still before Client review is a **delay** whatever its stage says, and is flagged to admins and managers. Derived on every read from the stage's position in its own workflow, never stored, so reverting a stage cannot leave a stale flag. Ships with the operations dashboard.
